- F-51 **A generic whose parameter is typed by a type variable that another argument fixes converts a narrower argument for that parameter, on both paths** (row 388, closed; F-73, "The compiled checker infers a generic's static arguments with coercion ..."; F-137, "Under `walk`, a generic call's static arguments are inferred with coercion ..."). Under walk, `scale(b, z)` and `scale(b, 3)` for `scale[\T\](b: Box[\T\], x: T)` and `b: Box[\RR64\]` instantiate `T = RR64` and convert, as `b.accept(z)` does (`ProjectFortress/tests/InferCoercionRungK.fss:38-39`). Compiled, `scale(bL, 3)` and `scale(bL, z)` for `bL: Box[\ZZ64\]` check and convert (`ProjectFortress/compiler_tests/InferCoercionShapes.fss:64-65`). `gf(NarrowOf(2), "two")` for `gf[\T\](x: Wide, y: T)` converts its `Wide` argument on both paths (`ProjectFortress/tests/CoercionGenericFnRungC.fss:25`, `ProjectFortress/compiler_tests/CoercionGenericFnCompiledRungC.fss:24`). So under walk the library's top-level `Vector`- and `Matrix`-scalar operators (`Library/FortressLibrary.fss:2476-2486`, `:2869-2879`) take a `ZZ32` scalar with an `RR64` container (`ProjectFortress/tests/InferCoercionRungK.fss:59-67`), and a numeral for such a parameter converts the same way. The container probe that first showed the refusal is `reviews/numeric-hierarchy-integration-review/row388-container-probe.md`; its programs are not re-run.

- F-52 **The flattening's three questions, measured by a clean worker** (`reviews/flattening-questions-ways.md`). *The empty sum.* One reduction per element type, reached from one generic declaration, is the library's own pattern (`BitXor`, `MINNUM`, `CompilerLibrary`'s `ZZ32Addition`, the specification's `SumZZ32`). A form with no argument cannot be overloaded by its result type alone. The library's way around it, a static parameter and a `typecase` over a `() -> T` witness, works on both paths (F-59, "The replacement for `SUM`'s and `PROD`'s catch-all, judged on both paths"). C4 needs a `ZZ32` zero for `matOffset(0)` (`run-c4/src/MicroGptFlat.fss:43`), `RR64` results for `SUM e`, `SUM vm` and `DOT`, and `RR64`'s own `MAX`. *Mixed widths.* On the flat library both paths take, for a generic call over mixed widths, the narrowest type that its arguments convert into, and convert them (answer 8's promotion; F-55, "Static arguments are inferred with coercion on both paths ..."). The two microGPT programs have no `ZZ64` variables, but they need `ZZ32`-to-`RR64` and literal-to-`RR64` coercions, which the compiler prelude lacks and the one library has; against it their `1.0 s` and `1 - beta1` are `RR64` on both paths (`reviews/numerics-plan-coordinator/evidence-B.md` § 7.1). *`RationalQuantity`.* The compiled checker refuses it ("Cyclic type hierarchy"), and walk cannot instantiate it; the library never had it or any sign-refined type. *The compiler prelude's flat tower* was flattened in 2009 and 2010, not by Steele in 2011: David Chase cut the subtyping between its widths (`6896886fb`, 2009-08-31), Sukyoung Ryu added its first exclusion, `ZZ32 excludes ZZ64` (`3e0306166`, 2009-10-16), and Jan-Willem Maessen and Eric Allen widened the exclusions and added the first coercions (`128f313b5`, 2009-11-17; `2ef730c88`, `d0cde7d17`, 2010-01). Steele built the comparisons and the operators on the flat prelude in 2011 (`b3c2e1342`, `da5291587`, `d535963cb`; the note's Question 1 § 7).

- F-55 **Static arguments are inferred with coercion on both paths: the compiled checker from the arguments' static types and the expected type, walk from the arguments' run-time types** (F-73, "The compiled checker infers a generic's static arguments with coercion ..."; F-137, "Under `walk`, a generic call's static arguments are inferred with coercion ..."; the mechanism in `reviews/numerics-plan-coordinator/evidence-B.md` sections 3.2, 3.4 and 4.2). The checker's attempt by subtyping solves the constraint "argType <: domain", with "range <: context" added when a context is given (`ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/scala_src/useful/STypesUtil.scala:941-955`). A variable with a named lower bound takes the join of its lower bounds, a union (`scala_src/typechecker/Formula.scala:585`, `scala_src/types/TypeAnalyzer.scala:80-81`), with one heuristic, a single lower bound's ancestors (`Formula.scala:586-604`); the promotion then instantiates a union at the narrowest named type, the parameter's bound among the candidates. A variable that no lower bound fixes takes the intersection of its upper bounds (F-78, "The compiled checker instantiates a type parameter that nothing at a call fixes ..."). `checkApplicableWithInference` is documented "with static argument inference and no coercion" (`scala_src/typechecker/impls/Functionals.scala:178-179`), and the coercion attempt is `checkApplicableWithCoercion` beside it; the coercion oracle targets only trait, tuple and arrow types (`scala_src/typechecker/CoercionOracle.scala:104`, `:170-182`). Walk's unification, which joins two lower bounds to their least common supertype (`interpreter/evaluator/types/FType.java:350-381`), is its rule's fallback, `inferByUnification`. The specification's coercion chapter says "types named by type parameters *do not have coercions*" (`Specification/basic/conversions-coercions.tex:366-368`), and its inference chapter admits every argument against the inferred instance's parameter types by substitutability, a coercion included (`Specification/basic/inference.tex:119-126`; F-173, "The specification's type-inference chapter states the rule the compiled checker builds"). A numeral and a `ZZ32` for a declared `ZZ64` parameter of a generic function whose static argument is inferred check and convert compiled, `scale64(bS, 3)` and `scale64(bS, z)` (rows 401 and 388; `ProjectFortress/compiler_tests/InferCoercionShapes.fss:62-63`). A mixed-width call takes the narrowest type its arguments convert into: compiled, `pick(3, l)` with `l: ZZ64` is `ZZ64,ZZ64` (`InferCoercionShapes.fss:67`), and under walk `twiceK(l, z)`, under an `Integral[\T\]` F-bound, is a `ZZ64` (`ProjectFortress/tests/InferCoercionRungK.fss:45`). The compile path gives every empty `extends` clause `Object` (row 412), so a call that fixes such a parameter by nothing else, where `()` or another type `Object` excludes is expected, is refused there (`Specification/basic/inference.tex:290-297`). Walk gives such a parameter `Any`, the bound of an empty `extends` clause, where it instantiates the declaration a call runs (`instanceOf`, `ProjectFortress/src/com/sun/fortress/interpreter/evaluator/EvaluatorBase.java:149-208`; row 424), and leaves at `BOTTOM` the kinds that F-141, "Under `walk`, a type parameter that nothing at a call fixes takes its declared bound ..." names. Walk converts no body to its declared return type (row 387), so `inc(u)`, generic over `Integral[\T\]` and adding a numeral, returns `6 : Long` for `u: NN32`, where the checker types the call `NN32` (`evidence-B.md` section 4.3). The checker passes the expected type at a typed binding, a declared return type, a block's last expression and the branches of an `if … else`, keeps it at a call written `f(x)`, a method invocation and an operator (F-56, "Keeping the expected type at a call written `f(x)` ..."), and drops it at an argument of another call (row 455; F-87, "The compiled checker gives a call its expected type ..."). Walk has no static context: a typed binding converts a call's result after the call, so `i: ZZ64 = idK(3)` answers `3 : ZZ64`, and it cannot fix a static argument that only the result mentions (row 510).

A call dispatched by size (c)" · from `explorations/reviews/size-runtime-design-brief.md`, "Run-time cost, measured" · commit `a77ccbee1` · by consolidation chunk F2a: cut to the form. The 8.6 MB of classes, the ldc constant, the instanceof and table look-up by arm kind, and where only the JVM's bounds check runs, with their lines, to size-cost.md sections 2 to 5; the ZZ32Vector constructor's load failure to row 408 and its expected failure XXXVecCtorLoad.

- F-40 **What a size costs at run time, measured: a few kilobytes at load, nothing on the hot path, and only the overload dispatcher ever reads a size off a value** (`perf-probes/nat/size-cost.md`; `reviews/size-runtime-design-brief.md`, "Run-time cost, measured"). Sizes add a few kilobytes to the 8.6 MB of classes that a run loads, and start-up does not change beyond noise. The stamped method of a sized object holds its size as a constant (`ldc 8`; `size-cost.md`, "The answers"). A loop bounded by that constant is no faster than one that reads the storage's length: the cost is boxing and a transaction check per local variable, about 31 ns per element, and nothing ties the size to the storage's length. The dispatcher tests a literal-size arm by one `instanceof` on the stamped class; for an arm generic in a size it reads the size's descriptor and looks the arm up in a table on every call. A dispatched call costs about 4 to 20 ns more than a resolved one. The checker checks a shape once per call site at compile time (`DOT`'s shared inner size, `Library/FortressLibrary.fsi:1764-1765`). At run time a shape is checked once per arm tried at a dispatched call and once per type test, and never at load, in a resolved call or per element, where only the JVM's own bounds check runs (`compiler/runtimeValues/FZZ32Vector.java:64`). No optimisation uses a size. A `ZZ32Vector` passed to a generic object's constructor fails at load with no size involved (row 408, expected failure `compiler_tests/XXXVecCtorLoad`).

Why a dispatcher needs the change, the fix's 43 lines and MieDispatchSub's answer to scope-call-site-dispatch.md sections 2 and 3; the 329 symbolic operator pairs, which priced the specification's former overload sentence that P-45 dropped, to its section 5; the patents' columns and lines to patents-forest-rule.md section 1; the Fortress group's keeping of the rule after 2012 to F-166 and P-43; the languages by name to literature.md; the dates of the specification's sentences to spec-sentences-dated.md.

- F-45 **The exclusion fork, priced three ways and dated** (five notes under `reviews/mie-probes/`). These are the prices on which route A, keeping the rule, was chosen; route A is built (P-43, "The exclusion rule stays, and the number tower is ..."). Dropping the rule needs every dispatcher's arms to use the dispatcher's own static arguments, since a dispatcher is loaded under the call site's static arguments and only its arms read the value (`scope-call-site-dispatch.md`). That fix, 43 lines in `OverloadSet.java`, is built for template dispatchers, and not for dotted and functional methods or for `opr` and `nat` arguments. With it and the rule relaxed, `MieDispatchSub`, which the checker refuses as a double member of `Tag`, compiles and answers `Marker[Blue]`. Symbolic operators, which the overloading checker never examines, would add 329 pairs. The 2012 patents' forest rule lets a self-typed generic, one that "comprises exactly T", be carried at several instantiations that form a chain (`patents-forest-rule.md`; US 8,843,887 col. 8 l. 8-12, US 8,898,632 col. 8 l. 23-27). Alone it does not close the specialisation hole; route C, the next entry, completes it. In the literature (`literature.md`), the Fortress group kept the rule after 2012 (Welterweight 2012; POPL 2019, whose [Anc-Same-Trait] is the rule, blanket for an invariant parameter; F-166, "The team's latest word on types and on the exclusion rule is the unfinished restart, not `Specification/`"). Java, Kotlin, Scala and X10 keep the rule with flat numbers; C#, F#, Rust and Haskell allow double membership and choose statically; Julia avoids it by single inheritance and gives up static soundness. No one has published a resolution of double membership under run-time dispatch, nor a proof that the rule is necessary. The dates of the specification's sentences on the rule are in `spec-sentences-dated.md`.

Reversing route A to C needs, on top of un-flattening the tower, the call-site fix in every dispatcher, a per-level method in the code generator, walk's work, a way past the overflow and the seven declarations (P-43, "The exclusion rule stays, and the number tower is ...").

- F-143 **Under `walk`, a type parameter whose bound mentions itself and that nothing at a call fixes is left open, an object inherits no declaration that a trait's `override` declaration overrides on every path, and an object expression is checked under the Meet Rule for Functional Methods** (`compile-ladder/rung-walk-open-param/REPORT.md`; row 424's F-bounded half and rows 614 and 618 fixed; rows 645 and 646 open). `EvaluatorBase.instanceOf` gives such a parameter `BottomType.OPEN` (`interpreter/evaluator/EvaluatorBase.java:184-194`). The open type is below every type, and every value matches it where walk checks a value against a type: `typeMatch`, `as`, `asif`, a `typecase` clause and a typed local. So a reduction that writes no static argument runs on its elements' types, and `ilkName` prints such an instance as `BoxU[\OPEN\]`. Only the open type itself admits every value: a tuple, arrow or generic type that holds it admits no more than at `BottomType`, so `(Int, Int)` is not below `(OPEN, OPEN)` at dispatch, and an overload over `Any` runs for a pair beside one over `(T,T)` (row 663, contested; `tests/OpenTupleDispatchWalk.fss`). Being below every type, a declaration whose parameter type is the open type is the most specific one that applies, so it is chosen over the others for every argument, also one that no declaration admitted at `BottomType` (`compile-ladder/rung-walk-open-param/SKEPTIC.md`, `SkOpenDispatch`). A big operator's plain-bounded static parameters stay at `BottomType` (decision D2 of `compile-ladder/rung-walk-instance/decision-record.md`). `Constructor.overriddenInTraits` reads `override` per type, as the load check does (row 615). `BuildEnvironments.finishObjectTrait` runs `FunctionalMethodMeets` on an object expression without static parameters; one in a generic function takes the function's static parameters and is checked as a generic object is (row 647). Gated by `tests/UnwrittenReductionWalk.fss`, `InferUnfixedFBoundedWalk.fss`, `OverrideInTraitWalk.fss` and `FunctionalMethodMeetObjectExpressionWalk.fss`; the residues by `XXXUnwrittenEmptyFloatSumWalk.fss` (row 645), `XXXComprehensionTypedBindingWalk.fss` (row 646) and `XXXUnwrittenSetBigOperatorsWalk.fss` (row 662, a generic type that holds the open type). Constructor.checkOverrides, BuildEnvironments.checkGenericOverrides, symbolicInstance, their callers and ExprFactory with their lines to the report's section 1; that the library's load builds no stand-in to its section 4; the tests by name and row 693's expected failure to the report and the row; rows 653's and 665's halves to the rows.

- F-147 **Walk reads an `override` by the chapter's strict subtype, and checks the `override` of a trait, object or object expression with static parameters once, at its declaration** (`compile-ladder/rung-walk-override/REPORT.md`, `compile-ladder/rung-walk-override/SKEPTIC.md`; row 653's walk half, row 665's override half). `Constructor.checkOverrides` counts an inherited declaration as overridden only where each of its parameter types is a subtype of the override's and not all are equal (each a subtype of the other's) (`interpreter/evaluator/values/Constructor.java:569-632`). So an `override` at the inherited declaration's own parameter types is refused at load: '... does not override any inherited declaration'. `BuildEnvironments.checkGenericOverrides` checks a generic declaration that declares an `override` through the stand-in of `symbolicInstance`, each static parameter a symbolic type below its bound (`interpreter/evaluator/BuildEnvironments.java:886-899`). It is called at `:592` and `:875`, and at `interpreter/env/ComponentWrapper.java:186`. The one library's load reads its generic declarations for the modifier and builds no stand-in, since none declares an `override` (REPORT section 4). At an instance of a generic declaration, such as a generic trait's instance above an object, equal parameter types still count as overridden (`Constructor.java:591`, `:599`). Where walk does not read a static parameter's bounds, an inherited declaration whose parameter types mention a static parameter, and are not equal to the override's, counts as possibly overridden: in an object expression, whose static parameters walk's rewrite lifts without the where clauses of the declarations around it (`nodes_util/ExprFactory.java:1127`), and in a declaration that extends a type under a `where` clause, which walk reads as unconditional (`interpreter/evaluator/values/Constructor.java:600`, `:634-666`). Gated by `tests/OverrideEqualTypesWalk.fss`, `OverrideNothingGenericWalk.fss`, `OverrideNothingGenericTraitWalk.fss`, `OverrideNothingGenericObjectExpressionWalk.fss`, `OverrideGenericInstanceEqualTypesWalk.fss`, `OverrideObjectExpressionWhereBoundWalk.fss` and `OverrideConditionalExtensionWalk.fss`; the residue, an object expression's `override` over an unbounded static parameter loading, by `XXXOverrideObjectExpressionOverParamWalk` (row 693).

Of the port, `main` still carries one running piece, the `FTypeArrayList` fix for the JDK 8 `TreeSet` crash, which `clean-ladder` makes in two lines, beside dead files, cosmetic lines and the tracked generated sources; `lineage.md` lists them for removal, and the files that repeat the older account of this lineage. `main` carries `clean-ladder`'s handling of `default_repository/caches/global.map`, untracked and ignored (F-100, "`ant compileAll` deletes the caches folder, ...").

The restart's abandonment in 2012, its dates, author and size, the Working Draft's covariance, Bottom inexpressible, the PDFs' dates and the README's wording to spec-lineage.md sections 3 to 5 (the 1.0 PDF also in F-163); the paper's citation, licence and talk transcript to the extract's header and protocol.md; its static inference, the run-time return-type check, Naden's two write-ups, the default bounds, Theorem 4.1, [Anc-Same-Trait], [D-Method] and the exponential dispatch to the extract's sections 2 to 7; the instance rule's wording to P-48 and F-78; the paper's lack of closed types, nominal exclusion, modules, top-level functions, coercions and numbers to the extract's section 6.

- F-166 **The team's latest word on types and on the exclusion rule is the unfinished restart, not `Specification/`** (`coordinator/spec-lineage.md`). `Documentation/Specification/` was begun 2012-02-20 and abandoned 2012-05-31 with a 65-page build and mostly skeleton chapters; it was never adopted. Its Types chapter (`Documentation/Specification/Prose/Language/types.tick`, Luchangco, 2012-05-31) is from-scratch prose written a year and a half after `Specification/`'s last edit. It restates the rule as "instantiation exclusion" (`:353-376`), with a `\note` giving the stronger imposed form: a trait extending two instantiations must extend an expressible one below both. It adds a `covariant` modifier on trait static parameters (`:320-339`), where the Working Draft expresses covariance with a self-extending `where` clause (`Specification-1.0-frozen/basic/trait-parameters.tex:339-351`), and it says `Bottom` is inexpressible (`:173-195`). On static parameters, traits, exceptions, components and the numeric library, `Specification/` is the only statement. "July 19, 2012" on the specification's PDF is the date of the parentless import `5a68404fd`, not of any edit. `fortress.1.0.pdf` is the team's own 2008 artefact (`403afbe0b`), and the root `README.md:105-107` calls the directory beside it "the frozen Fortress 1.0 specification", which only the PDF is. Later than both is the type group's last published word on instantiation and dispatch: Park, Hong, Steele and Ryu, "Polymorphic Symmetric Multiple Dispatch with Variance", PACMPL 3, POPL 2019, Article 11 (`research/extracts/ParkPOPL2019-extract.md`; the full text beside it, `ParkPOPL2019.pdf` and `ParkPOPL2019.txt`, CC BY-NC 4.0, the open-access exception to the rule on copyrighted PDFs in `explorations/protocol.md`; the talk's checked transcript, `research/extracts/ParkPOPL2019-talk.md`; the curator's decisions rechecked against it, `reviews/decisions-review/popl-recheck.md` and `reviews/array-judgements-popl-check.md`). At run-time dispatch a type parameter that nothing fixes takes the intersection of its upper bounds, its declared bound and the constraint of the call's static return type, and is never instantiated at `Bottom` (p. 11:19). That instance rule is the curator's decision (P-48, "A type parameter that a call's arguments do not fix takes its bound, never `Bottom`."), and the compiled checker builds it at a call (F-78, "The compiled checker instantiates a type parameter that nothing at a call fixes ..."). Of static inference the paper asks only that it instantiate at non-`Bottom` types (pp. 11:16-17), gives no algorithm (p. 11:25), and annotates its one static example, `O.m(3, true)`, at the union `Int ⊔ Boolean` (p. 11:21). The run-time check of the return type chooses the instance, never the declaration (Lemmas 5.8 and 5.9, pp. 11:22-23), as Naden's second write-up proposes (`Papers/RuntimeInstantiation/RTRinstantionTheory.tex:73-80`), not by the static context limiting which declarations apply, which his first rejected (`Papers/Types/journal/justificationOfRTR.tex:241-305`). A type parameter's default bounds are `Any` and `Bottom` (p. 11:9). No method type parameter may appear in an upper bound (p. 11:11), so the paper's soundness theorem does not cover the library's F-bounded functions, `SUM`, `BIG MAX` and those over `Integral[\I\]`. Theorem 4.1 keeps static uniqueness, a unique most specific declaration for every call (p. 11:15). The exclusion rule is [Anc-Same-Trait], a class that extends two instances of a trait extends an instance below both (p. 11:11), blanket for an invariant parameter. [D-Method] keeps a covariant parameter out of parameter positions, so no self-typed algebra trait can be covariant (`Equality[\+T\]`; the recheck's § 1.1). The paper accepts that its dispatch may blow up exponentially in some cases, a trade-off against expressiveness (p. 11:24). It has no closed types, nominal exclusion, modules or top-level functions (pp. 11:3, 11:25), and no coercion or numbers.

- F-176 **The specification allows arithmetic in a size, and two size expressions are the same type when they are written alike once numerals are folded** (`Specification/appendices/grammars/concrete-syntax.tex:533-547`, `:675-681`; `Specification/basic/expressions/constant.tex:15-21`; `Specification/basic/types-vals-vars.tex:49-62`; `Specification/appendices/internal-document.tex:167-199`). A static argument may be a numeral, a name, a sum, a difference, a product, a power with a numeral or a bracketed expression, and denotes its number. Types are identical when their static arguments are; two size arguments are identical when they are written alike once each operation on numerals is replaced by its value, and a static parameter is not known to differ from a numeral (`types-vals-vars.tex:51-62`; Appendix I, "When two sizes are the same", `Specification/appendices/changes.tex:3224`). The team's note beside the identity sentence, "It isn't complete in any case", stays (`:49-50`); the 2012 Types chapter drops the sentence; the team's Q&A refuses `T[\n+1\]` beside `T[\0\]` (`internal-document.tex:167-199`). The compiled checker computes each operation on numerals, refusing as out of range a power it does not compute, and compares other size expressions by their written form (F-146, "The compiled checker accepts arithmetic in a size ..."; P-55, "A size may hold ..."). Walk computes `+`, `-` and juxtaposition at instantiation and stops on a power (`interpreter/evaluator/EvalType.java:468-480`; row 676), and binds only a name or a numeral at a call (`interpreter/evaluator/types/IntNat.java:133-146`). The compiled path computes a size when it names a class (`compiler/NamingCzar.java:1904-1905`, `runtimeSystem/InstantiationMap.java:393-394`). The specification's note on static expressions says the same (`constant.tex:15-21`).

- F-100 **`ant compileAll` deletes the caches folder, the linker's untracked `global.map` with it, when the implementation changed, and keeps it otherwise** (`coordinator/process-engineering/build-efficiency.md` sections 2, 3 and 5). `compileAll` depends on `compileCommon`, which depends on `checkCaches` (`build.xml:956`, `:504`): when the sources or the build directory differ from what the last successful `compileAll` left, it deletes `default_repository/caches` entire, `global.map` with it, the linker's persisted `RepoState` (`linker/RepoState.java:174`). At its end `compileAll` keeps a caches folder only if the `implementation.stamp` in it equals the new build's, a SHA-1 over the built implementation (`ProjectFortress/build`, `third_party`, `bin`, the repository configuration and the JDK's version); `ant cleanCache` (`:357`) deletes the caches whatever their stamp. A `compileAll` with nothing changed keeps the caches and takes about 4 s; scalac runs only when its inputs changed; and `testFast`, `testSystem`, `testOnly` and the per-corpus test targets build first. `global.map` is untracked and ignored (`.gitignore:25`), and no step restores it: the first lookup of a run that consults the linker (`repository/GraphRepository.java:347`, `:404`, `:514`) constructs `RepoState`, which writes an empty map, four zero bytes, wherever the file is missing (`initRepoState`, `RepoState.java:366-385`), into the caches folder that `ProjectProperties` recreates (`repository/ProjectProperties.java:304-314`). ### F-197

2026-10-13 19:58Z · rewritten · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: pointer to F-196, which left for the cloud-container skill, replaced by its home

- F-197 **A VM restart can bring the disk back to an earlier state, so only what is pushed is safe.** When it does, the local `main` ends at an earlier commit, and the commits made since are missing locally and present on `origin` (the reflog jumps from that commit to the next one made after the restart). The scratchpad's newer files, the repository's newer `tmp/` files and the transcript backup's later passes in its log are gone; the session's own transcript stays whole. Recovery: `git fetch`, then `git rebase origin/main` for the local commits made after the restart, then push. So the coordinator pushes after every commit, and a page's source lost from the scratchpad is rebuilt from the published page (`Artifact` `read`). Another restart kept the disk intact (F-196, "The VM can be restarted under a live session with the disk intact"); which restarts keep the disk is not known.

Recovery: `git fetch origin main claude/worker-brief-fable-vnnuv8`, `git checkout -b main origin/main`, then `explorations/experiment/setup.sh` (JDK 25, `ant`, the build, the transcript backup). Pushing `claude/worker-brief-fable-vnnuv8` does not help re-provisioning, so every push also goes to `blinded-fable` (`explorations/protocol.md`, hard rules, "Every push"). The resumed session can start from a short copy of its transcript, and the new container's first snapshot then commits that copy over the full transcript, which reaches GitHub if the snapshot's push succeeds. Two sessions must not back up to one branch: the later one's pushes are refused.

### F-194

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the cloud-container skill, references/hooks.md, "The transcript backup" and "The platform's git-check Stop hook"; the coordinator skill, SKILL.md, "The record: what goes where" and "The boot"

- F-194 The transcript backup fires on the main session's Stop, not on any agent's completion, so a session inside one long turn is not being backed up. Its Stop hook returns at once and leaves the work to a detached runner, which copies the transcripts, commits, pushes whenever the branch is ahead of its upstream, and packs. A pass takes about 10 s and writes one line to `/home/user/fortress-transcripts-blinded/.backup.log`, which keeps the last 200. The runner swallows every error and never holds up the session, so a failed push shows only in the log's `push=` field. A container that dies within about 10 s of a turn's end loses that turn's snapshot. The runner cleans only an `index.lock` at least 10 s old that no process has open; a stale `/home/user/fortress/.git/refs/heads/transcripts-blinded.lock` fails every later commit (`commit=failed`) until it is removed by hand. The copier, its state file, the split of a transcript over 64 MiB, and the snapshots once skipped while a detached `gc` held the lock: `coordinator/remote-container.md` § How the snapshot works and § Three traps, the commit `transcripts-blinded@974d9be7`, and the reference copies in `coordinator/transcript-backup/`. The platform's own Stop hook, `~/.claude/stop-hook-git-check.sh`, comes from the launcher's `--settings /root/.claude/launcher-settings.json` and is rewritten at every start of the process. It exits 2, which makes the session take another turn, whenever the tree has uncommitted changes, untracked files, or unpushed or unverifiable commits. At the curator's request it is made a no-op, the original kept in the session's scratchpad (`stop-hook-git-check.sh.orig`). It comes back at each restart of the process, and turning it off again is the curator's to do in manual mode, since the automatic permission check refuses to schedule it (F-210, "The session's automatic permission check refuses a step ..."). The coordinator's push after every commit covers what it protected (F-211, "No push loop runs ...").

### F-200

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "Interrupts and process stops"; SKILL.md, "The facts of this environment"; the fortress-repo skill, references/session.md, its term "interrupt"; the cloud-container skill, references/hooks.md, "The transcript backup"

- F-200 **Stopping a turn kills every background agent alive at that moment; a message sent while the coordinator is busy kills nothing** (`coordinator/interrupt-archaeology/judgement.md` § 1, from `coordinator/interrupt-archaeology/evidence.md`; session `fe616d40`, main transcript, `subagents/` and `subagents/workflows/`; session `bdff267d`, `transcripts` branch, `001.jsonl`). The kill is the turn interrupt, which the transcript records as "[Request interrupted by user]", with any tool still in flight recorded "User rejected tool use". It kills Workflow agents and `Agent`-tool workers alike, and no agent alive at an interrupt has survived one. A message or a `/context` command sent while the coordinator is busy is queued and delivered inside the running turn or as the next turn. A manual compaction between turns leaves a running Workflow alive: its agents keep writing through the compaction and go on to their results. The transcripts record the interrupt, not the control that sent it; in one case the curator's own words confirm the client's stop control. Not tested: an automatic compaction mid-turn, and an interrupt during the Stop hook while a run is alive. The rule: send messages at any time; compact between turns; do not stop a turn while the session is working, including the seconds after a reply while the transcript backup runs; after an accidental stop, say so and resume the run with `resumeFromRunId`, which keeps every agent that had finished (the next entry; P-127, "Check-ins and stops"). An `Agent`-tool worker killed by an interrupt cannot be resumed: a `SendMessage` to it is refused ("was stopped by the user and won't be resumed ... only launch a new agent if the user explicitly asks"), so it is relaunched only at the curator's explicit ask, and the new worker is told its predecessor's leftovers (session `fe616d40`, six workers stopped by one interrupt). A Fable worker told to read its predecessor's transcript was ended by the API's safety filter as it printed the transcript's tail (`reasoning_extraction`, the false positive of the `agent()` entry below), so a Fable worker is given the leftovers in the tree and no transcript.

### F-201

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "Interrupts and process stops" and its term "journal"; the fortress-repo skill, references/session.md, "After an interrupt or a stop of the process"

- F-201 **A restart of the session's own process kills its background runs, and `resumeFromRunId` then recovers every agent that had finished** (session `fe616d40`; the environment manager's log `/tmp/env-manager.log`). A restart of the session's process (`environment-manager task-run … --session-mode resume`), whether the platform's cap (F-202, "The platform stops the session's process ...") or a VM restart (the VM entry above), stops every Workflow run and every `Agent`-tool worker alive at that moment. The conversation, the session ID, worktrees, untracked files, transcripts and each run's journal survive, and so do shell scripts the workers had started in the background. So the run is resumed, not relaunched: the same script and arguments as at its launch, with `resumeFromRunId`. A finished agent's result is kept, an unfinished one runs from its start, and its worktree's leftovers are its starting point; a relaunch from the base restarts at the first stage instead. An `Agent`-tool worker continues from its transcript on a `SendMessage`. The resume reuses the longest unchanged prefix of the run's `agent()` calls in call order, so it needs the launch's arguments byte for byte and the same order of calls: a base given as a short hash where the launch gave the full hash misses the cache from the first call, and when cached stages resolve at once, a pipeline may call its next stage in another order than the live run did and miss from there. A run whose script has changed since launch is resumed from a copy of the script as launched (`git show <base>:<path>`). Where the call order would differ, the copy forces the live run's order, prompts unchanged, by wrapping `agent()` so that the finished calls are issued in the journal's order of their `started` lines. A copy that changes only what follows the cached prefix (a gate skipped, a commit's prompt extended) runs the changed call live and nothing before it. A stage stopped mid-work can leave its work in the tree. A commit stage killed before it had committed leaves its edits in the main tree, uncommitted, and a resume whose commit prompt names them (a changed prompt, so a new key) checks and keeps them and lands the batch. A gather killed mid-work leaves partial staged state, which is stashed and moved aside before the resume; the gather then runs again live from its start under its key, and the journal holds two `gather` lines under one `launched` line (`compile-ladder/climb-batch-6.5b/RECORD.md`, "A relaunched gather"). The run's journal is `journal.jsonl` in `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session>/subagents/workflows/<run>/`: one `launched` line, then a `started` line per agent call (`key`, `agentId`, `label`, `phase`) and a `result` line with the same key and the agent's structured result, none for an agent that returned nothing. A resumed run appends to the same journal, and an agent run live again under a key the journal holds gets a second `started` line (`coordinator/tools/journal-text.py` reads it). A new session cannot resume another session's run (`remote-container.md` § The 2026-09-18 restart, and § Recovering a session whose container died, step 5).

### F-202

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the cloud-container skill, references/stops.md, "What stops the process"; the coordinator skill, SKILL.md, "The record: what goes where" (the next process stop in the boot note)

- F-202 **The platform stops the session's process after about 12 hours 58 minutes of continuous running, and restarts it in resume mode within seconds** (the environment manager's log `/tmp/env-manager.log`). Every stop in the log is a `SIGTERM` from outside ("Received signal, shutting down gracefully", signal `terminated`), followed by "Claude Code stopped due to context cancellation" with the run's length, then "Set session mode for environment" `resume`. The runs the cap ends last 46,636 to 46,660 s; the rest are short runs of a few minutes to a few hours that end when the session goes idle, and VM restarts. The cap's stops are not a crash, not memory and not a message; they fall while the curator is talking because the process runs continuously only while he or a check-in keeps it busy. Any restart, a VM start included, resets the clock, so the next stop is the last start plus 12 h 58 min, and a check-in armed a few minutes after that time resumes what the stop killed; the last start and the next stop are the boot note's.

### F-204

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the fortress-repo skill, references/session.md, "The Bash tool and waits" and "Long commands"; the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "What a brief holds"

- F-204 **An agent that waits longer than the prompt cache lives writes its whole context again at every wake** (session `fe616d40`, `subagents/agent-af9b2c3ba93eb9c88.jsonl`, probe P1's worker, its usage records deduplicated by message id; its cost in `coordinator/process-engineering/p1-cost.md`). After a wait longer than the cache's life, the agent's next call writes its whole context, with no cache read; between waits its calls read the cache. The cache's life is in the API's usage records: every cache write of the main session is labelled one hour (`cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`) and every subagent's five minutes (`ephemeral_5m_input_tokens`), as the platform's own description of the main session's wake-ups says of the main session. A poll within the life reads the cache and renews it, so a wait in steps costs a cache read per step. So an agent's waits are capped at 270 s: `wait_for` waits at most 270 s (the `fortress-repo` skill's `references/session.md`, "Long commands"), and the batch script's prompts tell its agents to wait with it (`coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.js:1311`, `:1395`, `:1593`). An `Agent`-tool worker's brief carries the same cap, its long run polled in steps under 270 s, or the run handed to a script while the agent ends its turn.

### F-207

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the cloud-container skill, references/stops.md, "Check-ins across a stop"

- F-207 **A check-in cadence under an hour is a series of one-shot `send_later` check-ins armed together in one turn, not a cron trigger and not a re-armed one-shot** (a worker's search of session `fe616d40`'s main transcript). The scheduler's cron triggers take an hour as their shortest interval. `send_later` calls armed in one turn, each `{"at": ..., "name": "... check-in N", "message": ...}`, fire within a minute of each target, with no re-arming. The scheduler refuses more than about ten trigger creations a minute: the eleventh `send_later` of a series got "Trigger creation rate limit reached. Try again in 56s." A series ends when its last check-in has fired, so a run that outlasts it needs a new series, armed by the last check-in. The cadence is 45 minutes (P-127, "Check-ins and stops").

### F-208

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "The Workflow tool"

- F-208 **`agent()` in a Workflow returns null for an agent the harness marks failed, even after the agent delivered its structured output** (`coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.md`, "An agent that comes back with nothing"). When the API's safety filter blocks an agent's next message after it has returned its result through the structured-output tool (a false-positive `reasoning_extraction` refusal), the harness marks the agent failed (the run's `journal.jsonl`, `"type":"failed"`) and `agent()` returns null, though the agent's work may already be committed on its `wip/` branch. Every `agent()` call of the batch script goes through `callAgent`, which treats a null or undefined result and a thrown error alike and runs the role up to two more times, each retry told what the earlier attempt may have left (`ATTEMPTS` and `callAgent` in `coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.js`, the retry's head written by `retryHead` from the stage's `recover…` list).

### F-209

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "The Workflow tool"

- F-209 **`node --check` does not check the batch script as the Workflow harness parses it, so a spliced script is checked by parsing it as an async function body** (`coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.md`, "How the script is checked"). The harness runs the script as a function body, so two unescaped apostrophes inside a single-quoted string of `coordinator/climb-batch-workflow.js` passed `node --check` and were refused by the Workflow parser at launch. On Node v22.22.2, `node --check` exits 0 on a two-line file holding `export const meta = {}` and the same slip, and refuses the slip alone. The check that catches it: the script's text, with `export const meta` made `const meta`, handed to `new AsyncFunction(...)` (`AsyncFunction` being `Object.getPrototypeOf(async function(){}).constructor`), which throws "Unexpected identifier 's'" on the slip and accepts the script without it. `coordinator/tools/workflow-scenarios.js` parses the script this way and runs it once per scenario.

### F-212

2026-10-13 19:58Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home: the coordinator skill, references/delegating.md, "Which tier runs what"; references/analysis.md, "Measuring"

- F-212 **Fable draws on the all-models limits as well as its own, at a higher rate per token written than Opus** (session `fe616d40`'s transcripts, usage records deduplicated by message id, against the curator's readings of the usage panel). A Fable token counts against the all-models weekly limit and the five-hour session limit as well as Fable's weekly limit, at about three to four times an Opus token's share of the session limit per token written. The ratio comes from the session limit's readings over one window of 66 minutes in which two Fable workers ran beside the coordinator on Opus, against Opus alone before it; the weekly limits' readings, in whole percents, agree and are too coarse for a ratio. Not counted: output tokens (the transcripts' usage records undercount them), headless `claude -p` runs and any other session on the account. So a Fable worker spends the all-models pool too, and is not free when that pool runs low.

- F-214 **After a compaction, a page republished by its source path alone becomes a new artifact; republished with its `url` it keeps its link**: the map's page, first published from `scratchpad/coordinator-shape/page.html` before a compaction, came out at a new URL when the same path was published after it, and went to its own URL as a new version when the publish named that URL. So a page published before a compaction is updated by passing its URL, which the boot note keeps.

### F-197

2026-10-13 20:01Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home since 30a6acaef: the cloud-container skill, references/stops.md, "A VM restart that rolls the disk back"; the coordinator skill, SKILL.md, "The facts of this environment" and "Talking to the curator" ("Pages")

- F-197 **A VM restart can bring the disk back to an earlier state, so only what is pushed is safe.** When it does, the local `main` ends at an earlier commit, and the commits made since are missing locally and present on `origin` (the reflog jumps from that commit to the next one made after the restart). The scratchpad's newer files, the repository's newer `tmp/` files and the transcript backup's later passes in its log are gone; the session's own transcript stays whole. Recovery: `git fetch`, then `git rebase origin/main` for the local commits made after the restart, then push. So the coordinator pushes after every commit, and a page's source lost from the scratchpad is rebuilt from the published page (`Artifact` `read`). Another restart kept the disk intact (the `cloud-container` skill, `references/stops.md`, "What stops the process"); which restarts keep the disk is not known.

### F-198

2026-10-13 20:01Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home since 30a6acaef: the cloud-container skill, references/container-loss.md, "Re-provisioning"; the fortress-repo skill, references/committing.md, "Pushing"

- F-198 **A container can be replaced by a fresh one whose clone fails, leaving an empty repository** (the platform's failure `ref_not_found`). The platform shows "The requested branch or commit was not found in the repository". The new container has `/home/user/fortress` holding only an empty `.git` (on `master`, no commits), no other folders under `/home/user` (worktrees, base builds and the transcript worktrees gone), JDK 21 alone and no `ant`. Recovery: `git fetch origin main claude/worker-brief-fable-vnnuv8`, `git checkout -b main origin/main`, then `explorations/experiment/setup.sh` (JDK 25, `ant`, the build, the transcript backup). Every pushed commit survives; the running agents and their uncommitted work do not. A rebuilt container clones the branch the session was created from, `refs/heads/blinded-fable` for the coordinating session (its `session_context.sources`, read with `get_session`), so the clone fails when that branch is gone from the remote. Pushing `claude/worker-brief-fable-vnnuv8` does not help re-provisioning, so every push also goes to `blinded-fable` (the `fortress-repo` skill, `references/committing.md`, "Pushing"). The resumed session can start from a short copy of its transcript, and the new container's first snapshot then commits that copy over the full transcript, which reaches GitHub if the snapshot's push succeeds. The coordinating session's full transcript up to such a snapshot is kept at its own path on `transcripts-blinded`, `projects/-home-user-fortress/fe616d40-a9c6-56d7-9da1-7168a172765d.until-2026-10-08T15-41.jsonl.parts/`, and its later transcript goes on at the usual path. Two sessions must not back up to one branch: the later one's pushes are refused.

### F-199

2026-10-13 20:01Z · removed · by coordinator rewrite, step 12: left for the skills, its rule at its home since 30a6acaef: the cloud-container skill, references/hooks.md, "The transcript backup", and references/container-loss.md, "Re-provisioning"

- F-199 **The transcript backup's Stop hook is registered in the tracked `.claude/settings.json`, and `setup.sh` adds it to `~/.claude/settings.json` only when the tracked file does not register it** (`.claude/settings.json:8`; `explorations/experiment/setup.sh:67-75`). A hook registered in both files runs two passes at each turn's end, about 4 s apart (the backup log's pairs), harmless because the lock serializes them. A rebuilt container comes up with no packages, no build, no backup worktree and no `~/.claude/settings.json`; `setup.sh` restores them in about 4 minutes.
